# OMP full accepted-programme audit and replacement goal

2026-09-18. Review only. No product implementation, operational/backlog mutation, grant change, publication, deployment, paid model call, or native-goal activation. One accountable reviewer. This audit replaces the **scope of the earlier continuation recommendation**, not the historical delivery, programme authority, work records or accepted decisions.

Reading order: A → C → D → E → F. B retains the complete reference inventory; load the relevant rows for a bounded work packet rather than copying the entire inventory into every worker prompt.

## A. Direct answers and authoritative baseline

| Question | Answer | Evidence and limits |
|---|---|---|
| Is every accepted MASTER obligation delivered? | **No.** | Full ECC activation, typed intake/exploration, managed adaptive research, empirical mechanism/recursive improvement, complete Fleet/knowledge/correction, coordinated Web/WFM and sustained qualifications remain. §B accounts for the scope; source and tests do exist for significant foundations. |
| Was the narrower old goal completed? | **Yes, as a reviewed and packaged source milestone.** | Actual native goal `885c4adb-80ef-4c5f-b8f3-4077d716e869`, thread `01a0b245-b374-7841-923c-7650ecc99a46`, is `complete`; objective explicitly says “first useful delivery,” retains later deliveries and both qualifications. Three exact source candidates, independent review, executable evidence and package exist. It did not claim production activation or the whole programme. |
| Does that completed status cover the whole accepted programme? | **No.** | Broader older goals remain separately paused/usage-limited; full programme MASTER/GOAL-PROMPT says every retained core/Fleet/UI/ECC/research/qualification obligation must pass. Replacing a full-programme objective with a smaller goal was explicit, not evidence the remainder was done. |
| What can owner use today? | **Installed source-linked CLI, responding WorkService; inspectable/reconstructible reviewed patch and control candidates.** | Live WorkService responds to supported reads; CLI remains source-linked 18.0.6. OMP-280 fixes collection membership in reviewed source. No household Plex deployment or working integrated control installation was shown. WebUI has substantial pre-existing session functionality, but no listener on inspected 7490/7491 ports. |
| What is installed and proven? | **Older bounded runtime qualifications, not current integrated programme.** | Raw OMP-277 CI at `1b78b801` records 121 PostgreSQL tests and 22 installed cases, zero required skips. Matching manifest verified. Live service still runs from older mutable checkout; reviewed control client currently refuses `contract_mismatch`. Fleet storage/inference listeners exist; service availability is not full knowledge/research qualification. |
| Is the next goal only three fixes or the next package? | **No.** | Replacement goal covers every applicable unresolved row in §B through all §E phases. Packaging E1, finishing R04 or delivering one campaign is a checkpoint, not goal completion. |

Confidence: exact source identities, native readback, historical OMP-277 raw logs/manifest, archive integrity and freshly executed boundaries are strong. Current combined installation, hundreds of historical acceptance details, complete native programme adoption and comparative usefulness remain partly or wholly unverified. No percentage estimate or evening-duration inference is warranted.

Material supersession:

- Owner correction on September 18 removed the nonexistent `OMP_Astra_Implementation_Handoff.md` dependency and native `/now`/`/summary` prerequisites for Codex source development. Native product authority/acceptance is not abolished.
- Full programme requirements survived the smaller kickoff. “First” changes order, not final scope. Older Fleet-first and broad UI deferral do not block the already accepted thin monitoring/control slice; broad Web/Fleet live capabilities retain their own dependencies.
- External lead is Sol Medium, worker Gemini 3.8 Flash, planner Fable 5.1 when needed, reviewer Kimi, exceptional bounded Astra. Older native work criteria explicitly naming Fable publication gates and effective native audit policy are **not silently erased**. Resolve any conflict through supported scope/policy disposition before that gate; do independent development meanwhile.
- Historical optional desktop/public exposure/solver/proprietary donor proposals remain conditional. General CPK, NSI, Fleet, WFM, ECC and full research were retained accepted programme, not made optional by the previous review's sequencing preference.
- WebUI's older Linux-only/manual-version/no-self-update limits do not cancel later accepted coordinated cross-platform OWEB contracts. Earlier terminal teardown semantics must reconcile with later qualified detach/reconnect behavior.
- Canceled HOME-141/144/149 and OMP-47 are historical ownership records. Their state does not prove SQL-0/retirement failed or succeeded; current inherited guarantees remain. OMP-16's parked lineage needs supported reconciliation, not resurrection.

## B. Full requirement inventory and coverage

### B.1 Interpretation and counts

The matrix below is a **frozen audit inventory**, not an operational tracker. `N202.1`, `ECC2.3`, `CS08` and similar labels are audit-only references; existing operational IDs/owners appear separately. No new work item was created. One implementation/evidence set can satisfy overlapping rows if identities/protocols match; rows are not effort units, unique feature counts or percentage usefulness.

Dispositions:

- **V — verified satisfied at stated scope.** Historical accepted scope does not automatically qualify a changed release. Preserve/regression-check, do not rebuild.
- **P — partially satisfied.** Evidence and exact missing parts are named. Implement only missing parts after source reconciliation.
- **I — implementation evidence exists, required-level verification insufficient in this audit.** Includes preserved native DONE/historical Web claims whose complete primary proof was not re-adjudicated. Recover valid evidence/applicability first; do not infer defect or reopen status.
- **N — provisionally not implemented in inspected programme/control candidates.** Full specified capability not located, with explicit newest-source incompleteness. This is not proof no alternate worktree contains it; reuse search is mandatory before authoring.
- **B — concrete authority/operational blocker.** Independent source/preparation can proceed. Blocked work remains in full scope, never counted delivered.
- **D — explicit cancellation, deferral, conditional or unaccepted proposal.** Source decision and re-entry/exclusion are recorded. Accepted later work is not moved here merely to shorten goal.

| Disposition | Criterion rows |
|---|---:|
| V — Verified satisfied at named scope | 21 |
| P — Partially satisfied | 266 |
| I — Implemented/evidenced, verification insufficient | 169 |
| N — Provisionally not implemented in inspected candidates | 104 |
| B — Concrete operational/authority blocker | 11 |
| D — Explicit deferred/superseded/canceled/conditional exclusion | 36 |
| **Total** | **607** |

Rows needing completion or evidence/applicability resolution P/I/N/B: **550**. D rows remain explicitly retained with their conditions, not counted delivered. V rows do not imply whole-item completion.

Coverage contract: all79 current native OMP-202…280 items are represented, splitting all structured criteria and retaining description-bound obligations where structured arrays are empty. Full WP1–8 deliverables/acceptance, R00–19, all17 research complete-system clauses,35 accepted product decisions plus Q36, research negative matrix, NSI-1–6, CPK-0–6 and its17 qualification families, Fleet-1–15, WFM-1–12, OWEB-1–9, all28 original Web acceptance/parity rows,12 later Web parity capabilities and cross-cutting lifecycle/knowledge/economics/qualification requirements are represented.

**Unreviewed at full primary-evidence depth:** the I rows listed below, remaining acceptance portions of P rows, and runtime/promotion proof for N rows. Every such row has verification-first work in its phase. There are no uncategorized rows in this inventory, but this cannot establish an entire-roadmap-complete verdict. Whole historical log corpora, old missing originals and every private deployment were not independently requalified. A file existence check or green summary did not upgrade a row to V.

| N219.1 | WorkService OMP-219 revision 448302b0-f033-49cf-a81c-92a4a0ec2b7e | OMP-219; BACKLOG r1; --queue orders umbrella items before their child deliverables; no per-item skip | P | Candidate none; Native queue remains existing product path; description identifies umbrella ordering/whole-grant stop. | Verify dependency ordering and per-item skip through actual queue before enabling multi-item use. | E7; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Finding<br>Grant e2565df8 (2026-09-01, anchor OMP-218): after the anchor completed, ascending key order activated OMP-202 — the v9.1 umbrella program — ahead of the child items its criteria depend on (omp-webui CI workflow item, fork CI baseline item, OMP-209). OMP-202's sealed criterion 6 requires a green CI baseline on BOTH repositories, but omp-webui has no CI workflow; creating it is a separate queued child item and is cross-repo (unsealable under an oh-my-pi grant). The grant offers no per-item skip: the only exits from an unsatisfiable active item are burning close attempts to NEEDS_FIX or stop_execution, which skips ALL remaining queued items.<br><br>## Fix direction<br>- Queue ordering: schedule items with parent/umbrella relations after their children (or more generally, order by dependency before key order).<br>- Add a bounded per-item skip/defer action (skip_active_item) so one unsatisfiable item does not force stopping the whole grant.<br>- Queue admission could exclude umbrella items whose open children are also in the queue.<br><br>## Evidence<br>- Grant e2565df8-6f68-4b22-a30e-f0c7afdecb40: position 0 OMP-218 (completed+merged, PR #4), position 1 OMP-202 (umbrella, criteria_pending), omp-webui CI child at position 4, OMP-209 at position 3.<br>- omp-webui: `gh api repos/theturtlecsz/omp-webui/actions/runs?branch=master` returns no workflow runs; protection exists (approvals=1, enforce_admins, force/deletion blocked) but no status contexts can be required without CI.<br>- Prior stop receipt semantics (grant b1447381): stop skips all pending items (0 completed, 10 skipped). |

| N230.1 | WorkService OMP-230 revision d9859a29-3b8a-4fe4-ae3d-0352f742af55 | OMP-230; BACKLOG r1; Incorporate upstream 18.1.2 through the guardrail | I | Candidate none; Upstream pin/guardrail exist; programme candidates carry later fork work. | Check ancestry and release-specific proof first; no blind upgrade or model/config drift. | E9; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>The fork’s accepted upstream baseline remains 18.0.6. Upstream 18.1.2 is eight stable releases later and changes areas overlapping the workflow system and intentional fork behavior.<br><br>## Solution<br><br>After the standing guardrail is operating, incorporate upstream 18.1.2 at immutable commit `86bf72f52947f62ecaf9bd28e35572812e725a92`, retaining or re-fitting every intentional fork behavior and preserving upstream defaults elsewhere.<br><br>## Acceptance criteria<br><br>- [ ] The standing guardrail has passed for the complete 18.0.6→18.1.2 span with no unresolved incompatibility report items.<br>- [ ] Upstream commit `86bf72f52947f62ecaf9bd28e35572812e725a92` is an ancestor of the protected default branch.<br>- [ ] Every relevant upstream release change and every intentional fork behavior has one allowed classification, resolution, and concrete proof; no behavior is dropped without explicit owner approval.<br>- [ ] All repository gates pass with nonzero selected test counts.<br>- [ ] Rollback evidence is recorded and a newly started process passes fresh workflow-system checks after incorporation.<br><br>## Out of scope<br><br>- Releases after 18.1.2; the standing weekly process tracks those separately.<br> |
| N231.1 | WorkService OMP-231 revision 55e55589-6d50-44a3-a159-a77ce92bc90f | OMP-231; DONE r1; Workstation disk cleanup 2026-09-02: 188G of build caches purged; large-data candidates await owner ruling | D | Candidate 00abecf3-e8f2-550a-bb22-0a066bd5c9cd; Completed historical workstation cleanup, not a remaining product capability. | Preserve retention constraints; no repeat cleanup or ownership inference. | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Finding<br><br>Root drive hit 99% full (5.4G free of 393G). Build caches were the cause.<br><br>## Removed (all regenerable, verified unmapped by any live process before deletion)<br><br>- `~/kavedarr/target` 144G, `~/planner/target` 11G, `~/oh-my-pi/target` 9.8G (cargo build output)<br>- Browser binaries/caches: ms-playwright 3.3G, puppeteer 2.7G, chromium 426M (re-download on next use)<br>- Package caches: npm 4.4G, gradle 4.3G, bun 2.5G, cargo registry+git ~2.5G, uv 1.5G, trivy 845M, yay 376M, go-build 199M, pip 120M<br>- Rustup toolchains not pinned by any repo: 1.89.0, 1.90.0, nightly-2026-07-28, bare nightly (~5.7G). Kept: nightly-2026-08-08 (oh-my-pi pin), 1.95.0 (kavedarr pin), 1.88.0 (default), stable.<br><br>Result: 181G used / 193G free (49%).<br><br>## Left untouched — candidates needing an owner ruling (~70G)<br><br>- `~/infra/CRANKGIFS` 15G (media)<br>- `~/me5-evidence` 14G (TV-project proof archives)<br>- `~/Android` 9.7G + `~/.android` 8.1G (SDK/emulators — used by household TV testing)<br>- `~/notebooklm-client` ~9G (mostly root-level generated files)<br>- `~/media-discovery-work` 8.7G (old TV-app lane folders)<br>- `~/.codex/sessions` 4.1G (session transcripts)<br>- ~20 `~/kavedarr-*` lane folders ~3.5G<br>- `~/db-backups` 3.1G, `~/backups`, `~/parked` (backups — recommend keeping)<br>- `~/.omp/wt` 3.4G — execution workspaces; the OMP-228 one holds the only copy of the hand-resolved 18.1.2 merge conflicts and must stay until that re-run lands.<br><br>## Verification<br><br>- Pre-delete `/proc/*/maps` scan: zero processes mapping any target tree.<br>- Post-delete: all three target paths absent; `df` confirms 393G/181G-used/49%. |
| N232.1 | WorkService OMP-232 revision 807ad938-05f8-4c22-9713-fab5ed861107 | OMP-232; BACKLOG r1; Workspace open: expand ~ in paths and surface open errors in UI | P | Candidate none; Fresh WorkspaceBoundary probe rejects ~/media-discovery but accepts absolute path; Sidebar handlers have no visible rejection handling. | Fix shared path resolution and visible error boundary; prove actual browser open and failed-open message. | E1; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br>Typing `~/media-discovery` in the sidebar's "Open workspace by path" input and pressing Enter (or clicking the Open workspace button) silently does nothing.<br><br>Two defects compound:<br>1. The daemon's `workspace.open` handler (`packages/daemon/src/server.ts`) passes `payload.root` verbatim to `WorkspaceBoundary` with no tilde expansion, so `~/media-discovery` fails with `workspace root does not exist: ~/media-discovery` — even though the sibling `path.complete` command DOES expand `~` and round-trips `~/` in its completion results, teaching users the daemon understands tilde paths.<br>2. The web sidebar's `openRoot` handler (`packages/web/src/components/Sidebar.tsx`) awaits `client.command('workspace.open', …)` without a catch or any error rendering, so the command error never reaches the user — the click/Enter appears dead.<br><br>## Reproduction<br>WebSocket `workspace.open` with `{"root":"~/media-discovery"}` → error `workspace root does not exist: ~/media-discovery`; same command with `/home/thetu/media-discovery` succeeds.<br><br>## Fix<br>- Expand a leading `~/` (and bare `~`) to the daemon's home directory in `workspace.open` before constructing `WorkspaceBoundary`, mirroring `path.complete`'s expansion.<br>- Surface `workspace.open` command errors in the sidebar UI (toast or inline message) instead of silently swallowing them.<br><br>## Acceptance criteria<br>- [ ] `workspace.open` with `~/some-dir` opens `$HOME/some-dir`.<br>- [ ] A failed workspace open shows a visible error in the UI.<br>- [ ] Daemon test covers tilde expansion in workspace.open. |

| N234.1 | WorkService OMP-234 revision b546c11c-a8b7-4f57-aa64-2cf591da2281 | OMP-234; CANCELED r1; Paused grants: inconsistent seal/stamp gating, no signal to execution session, misleading refusal text | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>When a grant is paused, execution-phase writes are gated inconsistently and the execution session is never told. Evidence from 2026-09-02, grant e6f6d087 (OMP-228), paused 09:57:58Z:<br><br>1. seal_execution_criteria at ~09:59Z SUCCEEDED against the paused grant (criteria_sha256 recorded) — seal does not check grant state.<br>2. stamp_execution_plan at ~10:20Z was refused with "no active execution grant" — stamp does check state.<br>3. The pause notice ("Execution grant paused due to owner message. Use /execute resume ...") was queued only in the INTERJECTING session (the omp-webui one). The execution session received no signal and spent ~20 minutes of planning research before discovering the pause via the stamp refusal. The refusal text itself ("no active execution grant") does not say the grant is paused or name the resume command.<br><br>## Fix<br><br>Make paused-grant behavior consistent and visible: (a) seal_execution_criteria and stamp_execution_plan apply the same active-state gate; (b) refusals against a paused grant say paused and name '/execute resume <KEY>' instead of "no active execution grant"; (c) on owner-interjection pause, deliver the pause notice to the grant's execution session (checkpoint delivery or next-turn notice), not only to the interjecting session. Acceptance: pausing a grant mid-planning makes the next execution-session write fail fast with a paused+resume message, and seal/stamp agree on the gate. |
| N235.1 | WorkService OMP-235 revision b00fc564-00f6-4f93-9de6-0b89e91803bd | OMP-235; CANCELED r1; stop_execution Next-command line scrapes work keys from prose instead of structured resequencing targets | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>stop_execution's terminal notice synthesizes its 'Next:' command line from work-key tokens found in the stop body text instead of from the actual resequencing targets. Evidence 2026-09-02, grant e6f6d087 (OMP-228 umbrella stop): the stop body opened with 'Umbrella activated before its children (OMP-219 stop-early class)' — OMP-219 being a precedent citation to a closed item — and named /execute OMP-229 then /execute OMP-230 as the real guidance. The rendered terminal notice said: 'Next: /execute OMP-219 then /execute OMP-228'. An owner following that line would re-execute a closed defect item and then the same umbrella that was just stopped for wrong sequencing.<br><br>## Fix<br><br>The Next line must come from structured data, not prose scraping: either accept an explicit next_commands field on stop_execution, or derive it from ledger structure (pending children of the stopped anchor in dependency order — here the active 'OMP-229 blocks OMP-230' edge gives the order directly), and never harvest keys from free-text bodies. Acceptance: stopping an umbrella grant whose body cites unrelated precedent keys renders a Next line naming only the anchor's open children in blocks-edge order. |
| N236.1 | WorkService OMP-236 revision 1b789167-e8bf-4188-ba88-a7b28d9ba24c | OMP-236; CANCELED r1; /execute resume on a terminal grant reports 'execution workspace identity is incomplete' instead of a terminal-state refusal | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>Running `/execute resume OMP-229` after the OMP-229 grant was stopped (2026-09-05) produced the error "Cannot resume: execution workspace identity is incomplete". The grant was terminal (state `stopped`, item `abandoned`), so resume is impossible by design — but the message misdiagnoses the situation as a workspace-identity problem and gives no next step.<br><br>## Root cause<br><br>`session-system/extensions/workflow/host.ts` (resume subcommand, ~lines 2564-2576): the handler resolves the anchor key and baseline BEFORE checking whether the grant is terminal. On a stopped grant `exec.activeItem` is null (item abandoned), so `baseline` is undefined and the identity-incomplete branch fires. The `status` subcommand already computes `isTerminal`; the resume path never does.<br><br>## Fix<br><br>In the resume handler, check `g.state === "stopped" \|\| g.state === "canceled" \|\| g.state === "completed"` first and refuse with the actual state plus the real next command (e.g. "grant 2d482588 is stopped; resume is impossible — relaunch with /execute <KEY>"), including the terminal reason's first line. Keep the identity check for non-terminal grants.<br><br>## Acceptance<br><br>- `/execute resume <key>` on a stopped/canceled/completed grant names the terminal state and the relaunch command; the 'workspace identity is incomplete' message appears only for genuinely incomplete identity on a live grant.<br>- Host test covering the terminal-grant resume refusal.<br><br>Evidence: grant 2d482588-4553-401d-8086-d7fddb449298 (OMP-229), stopped_at 2026-09-05T13:15:18Z, activeItem null, resume attempt hit host.ts:2574. |

| N239.1 | WorkService OMP-239 revision 4dc024d2-2dd9-4502-b250-5262c74594ef | OMP-239; CANCELED r1; /execute admission gate accepts a worktree HEAD ahead of main on another item's unmerged candidates (OMP-238 grant baselined atop OMP-229's stopped-grant commits) | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>The /execute admission gate's 'up to date with default' check passes when the worktree HEAD is AHEAD of origin/main on another item's unmerged frozen candidates. The OMP-238 grant (ff6657a1) was admitted in worktree ~/.omp/wt/execute-omp-238-ff6657a1...-387daaf with initial_git_baseline 158eaf9d11 — the tip of THREE unmerged 'session candidate: OMP-229' commits (a18c514c3d, 46a5d0bd33, 158eaf9d11) from OMP-229's stopped grant, while OMP-229 is BACKLOG.<br><br>## Consequence<br><br>Every freeze parents on HEAD, so the delivery PR (execution branch -> main) diffs the other item's full unmerged work (4,154 lines) plus the stamped paths, while the audit manifest covers only the new grant's diff range (baseline..candidate). Merging would land stopped, unaudited-for-this-grant work on main as a hidden side effect. OMP-238 had to stop_execution with zero close attempts despite all criteria being met.<br><br>## Fix direction<br><br>Admission should refuse (or warn and require a fresh worktree) when HEAD != origin/main tip and the ahead-commits are session candidates of a different work item's grant, e.g. detect 'session candidate:' subjects for a different key, or compare HEAD against origin/<default> equality rather than ancestor-only.<br><br>## Evidence<br><br>- OMP-238 grant ff6657a1 stop body (2026-09-05) carries the full probe evidence.<br>- git: origin/main ad580d7d is ancestor of HEAD 158eaf9d11; OMP-229 candidates pushed at refs/heads/execution/omp-229-2d4825884553401d8086d7fddb449298. |
| N240.1 | WorkService OMP-240 revision 7c53020e-d209-49e5-8d86-e773d287a256 | OMP-240; CANCELED r1; Execution grant remote ref can name the wrong work item | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br><br>The active single-item grant for OMP-237 reports `remote_ref` as `refs/heads/execution/omp-238-ff6657a1e49245edaedc212dab03c514`, while its active work ID, original request, criteria, and plan belong to OMP-237. The checked-out symbolic ref is separately named `execution/omp-237-ca732023a8304d6e99858ad4711fa534`.<br><br>## Impact<br><br>The execution driver cannot reliably identify the anchor item or delivery branch. Freeze/push may update a branch named for a completed, unrelated item and bind PR history to the wrong work item.<br><br>## Fix<br><br>Admission must derive and validate the grant remote ref from the active work item's primary alias and grant ID, reject alias/ref mismatches before criteria sealing, and keep the checked-out symbolic ref aligned with the recorded remote ref. |

| N250.1 | WorkService OMP-250 revision bf1c9082-184d-5b55-ad13-70d684a57134 | OMP-250; BACKLOG r2; Harbor evaluation integration: adapter, isolated fixtures, independent grading | D | Candidate none; Native BACKLOG; stabilization guide explicitly optional later Harbor adapter, not trusted-runner owner. | Retain dependency 246/245; re-enter only accepted optional evaluation tranche. R06 trusted evaluator remains mandatory elsewhere. | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Figured out myself<br><br>Handoff H0-H4, exit at M2 (zero real model spend). evals/harbor/ package with pinned deps kept out of OMP runtime imports; no new /eval command. RPC-mode adapter (docs/rpc.md, src/modes/rpc/) drives the normal /execute command path: startup/readiness/WorkService checks before any live request; RPC ack = request acceptance only — track events and authoritative workflow state to the scenario terminal condition; no injected progress prompts (owner-interjection pause would alter the measured behavior); scripted UI answers recorded, never blanket auto-approve; crash/restart with same session identity; evidence persisted before teardown. Isolation: workflow/config.ts client refuses non-loopback WorkService URLs — qualify a shared-network-namespace topology with separate filesystems; no Docker socket in the worker; admin/migration credentials, graders, and authoritative evidence off the worker filesystem. Separate verifier environment.<br><br>This child OWNS both H4 task fixtures: F1 structured acceptance amendment (seeded description-only amendment defect from the revise_work child's bug class; success = real service readback of the amended criteria, stale-preview rejection, revision invalidation, independent tests) and F2 durable execution continuation (seeded continuation acknowledgement/recovery bug from the durable-continuation child's crash boundary; success = controlled process kill at the reproduced boundary, resumed work, no duplicate effects, correct terminal service state). A fixture asking an agent to repair a seeded bug and a harness crash-injection test are different experiments — label which is scored; never attribute a harness defect to an implementation model. Grader validated on known-good, known-bad/broken, and tampered/mismatched results before any model trial; grader rejects missing or foreign evidence; expected failures demonstrating seeded bugs labeled as such. Owns the missing-RPC-approval control (no inferred approval; actionable blocked state). Remaining H4 invariant controls are allocated to M3 owners: tracked-deletion freeze, audited-head-changed refusal, and broken-auditor-during-owner-stop → W0 reliability child; evidence/idempotency controls → service-boundary child; crash/replay controls → durable-continuation child; subtask/auditor terminal-result → template child. Parity requirement: same command through interactive path and adapter → same authorization, routing, transitions, result. Fixture readiness depends on the revise_work and durable-continuation children (blocks edges): each defines its fixture's seeded-defect class and known-good repaired behavior.<br><br>Owner tightening (2026-09-06): completion boundary — this item finishes at the documented M2 adapter-and-fixtures milestone, with M3 explicitly tracked elsewhere: the M3 invariant controls stay on their allocated owners listed above, and full service-evidence qualification (which also depends on OMP-247 and the remaining invariant controls) is not claimed by this item. Serial order as proposed. H5 (model-comparison plan, budget, reporting, trials) is owned by follow-on OMP-252.<br><br>## Asking you<br><br>Nothing at M2. Live model comparisons (H5 pairs, metered subset) come back with an exact run plan and cost estimate for explicit budget approval.<br><br>## Leaving for later<br><br>H5 controlled model/workflow experiments — now explicitly owned by OMP-252 (unrelated to OMP-202/H5 observation-to-policy work); any trusted-runner receipt authority. |

| N255.1 | WorkService OMP-255 revision f2dd9ef2-59c7-40e9-ae22-ae91b1297297 | OMP-255; CANCELED r1; Execute re-review of unchanged tree deadlocks: uuid5 push-receipt collision then finalized-candidate 409 with phase-locked stamping | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br>An /execute remediation re-review cannot converge when the candidate tree is unchanged, and the failure then bricks the grant.<br><br>## Mechanism (observed live on OMP-254, grant 0e8b87cd, 2026-09-06)<br>- begin_execution_review on an unchanged tree reuses the finalized attempt-1 candidate (41eaa4c3).<br>- It re-derives the content-addressed push-receipt uuid5 from unchanged inputs.<br>- Insert collides: 400 UniqueViolation receipts_pkey (existing receipt 424301e4; new verification receipt 59de884c landed first).<br>- The 400 leaves the item in phase executing.<br>- In executing, stamp_execution_plan is refused (needs planning/remediating).<br>- Any tree change then 409s: stale_evidence finalize_candidate planned mismatch (planned candidate already kind final).<br>- The freeze had also already committed the new tree locally (adc27915) before the 409, mislabeling it Work-Candidate: 41eaa4c3.<br>- Net: no autonomous verb can mint a fresh candidate; only stop_execution + fresh grant recovers.<br><br>## Fix directions<br>- Make push-receipt ids unique per attempt (salt uuid5 with attempt/close-attempt counter) or upsert idempotently.<br>- On a failed finalize/review, roll the phase back to remediating so stamping can mint a fresh planned candidate.<br>- Do not leave a local candidate commit labeled with a stale candidate UUID after an aborted freeze.<br><br>## Acceptance<br>- A NEEDS_FIX remediation with zero code changes can re-run begin_execution_review and reach the auditor.<br>- A failed review call never leaves the item in a state where both stamping and reviewing are refused. |
| N256.1 | WorkService OMP-256 revision d033fabb-d157-4d2c-ae18-c3f3232d56ca | OMP-256; CANCELED r1; /execute cannot satisfy live-activation acceptance criteria (bootstrap self-delivery gap, seen on OMP-245) | D | Candidate none; Native CANCELED; preserve history/relations; cancellation does not establish delivery. | Do not revive this item. Absorbed behavior remains under current owner (234/235/236/239/240→233; 244→243; other canceled/fixture items require explicit current lineage). | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Finding<br><br>OMP-245 (grant cee3f936, 2026-09-06) stopped after 2/5 NEEDS_FIX close attempts because its sealed criterion required verifying the LIVE installed extension exposes the new behavior. Inside /execute this is circular: the engine merges the delivery PR only on audit PASS, while the live extension (~/.omp/agent/extensions -> deploy worktree) can only be refreshed from merged main (session-system/install.sh + session restart). The native auditor correctly refused the post-merge deferral twice. Any owner tightening of the form "completion includes live activation/verification" makes an item structurally unclosable by /execute.<br><br>Secondary: the immutable initial path seal blocked adding the auditor-demanded execution-grant amendment regression (session-system/tests/execute-cycle-smoke.ts) during remediation; widening re-stamp refused as designed.<br><br>## Verification<br><br>Grant cee3f936 stop body (2026-09-06) pins both audit receipts, the frozen candidate b34e1fb3747eef0a43d90fc0eb84ae5fa23c9c57 on execution/omp-251-38de73be754f4391acad300f4d7ca913, and the refused widening probe. Fix direction: either the execution engine supports a post-merge activation+verification phase before close, or intake/criteria doctrine records live-activation as a post-merge obligation (child item) instead of an in-grant gate. |

| N260.1 | WorkService OMP-260 revision 46fcdbb6-c7a1-4d26-ba49-b4782c082c5e | OMP-260; DONE r1; Workstation disk cleanup 2026-09-06: 148G of build caches purged (91% -> 52% used) | D | Candidate none; Completed historical workstation cleanup, not a remaining product capability. | Preserve retention constraints; no repeat cleanup or ownership inference. | X; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Finding<br><br>Root drive hit 91% full (339G used, 36G free of 393G). Cargo build caches and regenerable package/system caches were the cause.<br><br>## Removed (148G total reclaimed; all verified unmapped by live processes before deletion)<br><br>- `~/kavedarr/target` 40G (cargo build output, unmapped)<br>- `~/kavedarr-per-title-delete/target` 50G + `~/kavedarr-admin-foundation/target` 49G (cargo build output in lane folders, owner-approved deletion)<br>- Package caches: `~/.bun/install/cache` 2.5G, `~/.cargo/registry/{cache,src}` 2.5G, `~/.npm/_cacache` 298M, `~/.cache/uv` 67M<br>- System: `/var/cache/pacman` cleaned to 2.4G (~3G freed), `/var/log/journal` vacuumed to 276M (~3.7G freed)<br>- Rustup toolchains: all 4 installed toolchains (1.88.0, 1.95.0, nightly-2026-08-08, stable) are actively pinned or default; none uninstalled.<br><br>Result: 191G used / 183G free (52%).<br><br>## Left untouched — protected / owner-decision candidates<br><br>- `~/infra/CRANKGIFS` 15G (media)<br>- `~/me5-evidence` 14G (TV-project proof archives)<br>- `~/Android` 9.7G + `~/.android` 8.1G (SDK/emulators — TV testing)<br>- `~/notebooklm-client` 9.0G<br>- `~/media-discovery-work` 8.6G<br>- `~/.codex/sessions` 5.0G<br>- `~/.omp/wt` 16G (execution workspaces / worktrees)<br>- `~/db-backups` 3.6G, `~/backups`, `~/parked` (WIP / backups)<br><br>## Verification<br><br>- Pre-delete `/proc/*/maps` scan: zero processes mapping any target tree.<br>- Post-delete: target paths absent; `df -h /` confirms 393G total, 191G used, 183G free (52%).<br> |

| N262.1 | WorkService OMP-262 revision 8887ece5-13cf-4d38-9720-05a22cf39f41 | OMP-262; BACKLOG r1; stamp_execution_plan reports error but still appends plan receipt | I | Candidate none; Historic error-with-write/double candidate report; newer state/preflight/reconciliation code exists. | Reproduce supported manual-plan/execution-stamp sequence in disposable service; do not repeat on live grant. | E1; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Finding<br><br>Observed live 2026-09-07T16:24:28Z during OMP-247 re-approval: `work action:"stamp_execution_plan"` on OMP-247 (no active execution grant; item had a fresh manual /plan stamp at 16:15:43) returned an error banner — "STATUS: EXECUTION GRANT stopped (terminal — resume impossible) … no active execution grant" — yet the call still appended plan receipt d6ae7546 (payload_sha256 79d35fdf, identical to the /plan receipt's payload) and minted a second planned candidate 2627dce7-a0b4-5232-ae17-2405f3f033 on the same revision edc8f4cf. The subsequent owner /summary close attempt then bound to the new candidate 2627dce7 instead of the /plan candidate a29ed9a4, and a push receipt (91561fc4) appeared on it at 16:24:29.<br><br>Two defects: (1) tool result said error while a write landed — result/write inconsistency; (2) the execution-stamp path computes a different candidate id than stampPlan for the same (work, revision, plan hash), so a stray stamp silently re-binds the close-attempt basis.<br><br>## Verification<br><br>Repro: manual /plan stamp on an item, then call stamp_execution_plan for the same work with the same plan file; observe error result plus a new plan receipt and new candidate in the workflow view (`GET /v1/work-items/<key>/workflow`). |
| N263.1 | WorkService OMP-263 revision 65cad5c7-7f59-43e3-8778-ce80e7590e26 | OMP-263; BACKLOG r1; Clean up stale atomic-push comments in release.ts and ci.yml | I | Candidate none; Historical stale release-comment task; source release path has changed since report. | Inspect actual current comments; correct only remaining mismatch without runtime work. | E1; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | ## Problem<br>Comments in `scripts/release.ts` (lines 488–507) and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (lines 106–115) still describe the pre-branch-protection atomic main+tag push workflow instead of the PR-first branch staging and post-merge tag publication cutover.<br><br>## Solution<br>Update inline comments in `scripts/release.ts` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` to accurately describe the PR-first release branch workflow and post-merge tag publishing.<br><br>## Acceptance criteria<br>- [ ] `scripts/release.ts` comments describe PR-first release branch push and post-merge publish.<br>- [ ] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` comments describe tag/dispatch triggers and metadata detection accurately. |

Only after ALL primary evidence, rider, cancellation and satisfied-child proof validation, and BEFORE the first completion-state mutation, may a locked predecessor in this exact fully validated batch be treated as its planned CANCELED post-state (cancellation proof) or DONE post-state (validated satisfied-child proof or sealed-rider proof). Caller-supplied cancellation/child IDs or unvalidated rider membership never exempt a predecessor. Missing or unreadable predecessors always refuse. Other current states, including lowercase or unfamiliar spellings, refuse unless its complete_work batch proof satisfies every existing validation and the validated atomic terminal post-state rule applies. Canceled nonblocking never proves delivered scope or replaces completion evidence. Ignore archived for predecessor eligibility as existing rules do; preserve own-item and rider archived checks. |

Admission compares current sorted source IDs to caller snapshot; activation compares current, caller-expected and stored source IDs; host recovery and both execution-bound completion routes compare current source-ID set exactly to immutable active_blocker_ids. Ordinary non-execution complete_work enforces current predecessor state plus the validated same-batch terminal post-state rule, with no invented seal. The same existing batch DTO/rule applies to execution-bound complete_work; its exact sealed source-ID-set equality still holds and a projected terminal post-state cannot excuse edge drift. Parent relations are not blockers. No predecessor historical revision/state or relation-record identity binding is added. Execution-bound complete_work must resolve its matching sealed execution_grant_items row by existing grant/work binding. Missing, unreadable or inconsistent execution binding refuses completion_blocked; lookup failure never reclassifies an execution attempt as ordinary and never substitutes an empty seal. Consistent execution binding means authorization_kind is execution AND execution_grant_id is present. Consistent ordinary binding means authorization_kind is summary or legacy AND execution_grant_id is absent. Every other combination refuses completion_blocked. The execution lookup is a PLAIN SELECT, without FOR UPDATE/FOR SHARE, scoped by workspace/grant/work inside complete_work's SERIALIZABLE transaction, reusing existing uniqueness and immutable seal. Missing row or malformed/NULL observed seal refuses; legitimate [] passes only against an empty current source set. No invented duplicate-row requirement or grant/lease enforcement. |
| N271.3 | WorkService OMP-271 revision 75d2b19a-d0fc-5bcb-8aac-bc28b05a37b9 | OMP-271; BACKLOG r2; Preserve terminal predecessor edges across recovery and both completion routes | P | Candidate d9db578a-b83f-59ae-9c77-f01175512746; Terminal predecessor source and real service/installed test cases exist in programme candidate. | Rebind exact post-state, source-ID seal and concurrency/recovery evidence; preserve edges and original criteria. | E1; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | Implement one service observation/helper at all four service sites: begin_execution, activate_execution_item, complete_execution_item and complete_work. Preserve route-specific errors: admission/activation invalid_request and existing snapshot/unfinished diagnostics; execution completion completion_blocked; ordinary completion refused completion_blocked. | CPK-BASE | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-BASE | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | P | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-01` \| The authority registry assigns each durable domain to exactly one native owner: WorkService/PostgreSQL for Work/NSI/Fleet, JSONL/session authority for interactive Chat, Git/filesystem for source state and Fleet controller for scheduling/process/worktree state. \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-02` \| Attempts to create competing durable truth through plugins, event middleware, projections, WebUI, Agent Hub, Fleet Manager, Auditor, Advisor, Observer or model output fail closed. \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-03` \| The workflow permits only `/intake -> /plan -> implementation -> real verification -> fresh independent /summary -> bounded fixes with complete reverification/reaudit -> /done`; skips and reordered transitions are rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-04` \| Product meaning, ratification, exceptions, consequential effects, publication, delivery grants and closeout remain owner/native-policy powers and cannot be delegated by configuration or plugin registration. \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-05` \| Conflicting fixtures prove the authority-precedence matrix selects the declared native source and never cache, projection, plugin or model-derived state. \|<br>\| `CPK-BASE-06` \| Gate orchestration proves H0-first, read-only H1/SQL-0 overlap, H2/H3 WorkService-mutation blocking until `SQL-0: PROVEN` and unchanged SQL -> NSI -> Fleet dependency order. \| |
| CPK-MAN | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-MAN | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-01` \| Manifest schema/version validation rejects missing required fields, unsupported versions, invalid types and unrecognized authority-sensitive fields. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-02` \| Canonical normalization produces byte-identical serialization and digest for semantically identical manifests regardless of source formatting or key order. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-03` \| Discovery produces the same ordered inventory regardless of filesystem enumeration order, locale, current directory or environment-variable ordering. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-04` \| Duplicate, case-confusable, Unicode-confusable, reserved or namespace-colliding plugin identities fail before graph construction. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-05` \| Every capability, event subscription, effect ceiling, scope, dependency, lifecycle hook and resource limit must be declared; undeclared use is rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-06` \| A resolved manifest is immutable for the boot epoch; mutation or digest drift requires a new resolution/boot and cannot alter an active authority path. \|<br>\| `CPK-MAN-07` \| The complete v9 extension inventory has one shadow manifest per component, default-off activation and parity evidence showing inventory collection itself changes no runtime behavior. \| |
| CPK-GRAPH | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-GRAPH | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-01` \| Every hard dependency resolves to exactly one compatible node and version before activation. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-02` \| Missing, incompatible or ambiguous hard dependencies fail closed with the requesting node and constraint identified. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-03` \| Cycles are rejected with a deterministic minimal cycle path suitable for operator diagnosis. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-04` \| Optional dependencies may be absent without silently activating substitutes or changing unrelated nodes. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-05` \| Topological order and graph digest remain identical across input permutations. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-06` \| Sealed/native nodes cannot be replaced, shadowed, wrapped or made ambiguous by a plugin declaration. \|<br>\| `CPK-GRAPH-07` \| Shadow comparison explains every node, edge, exclusion and order difference from legacy discovery and proves default-off graph construction has no behavioral effect. \| |
| CPK-CAP | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-CAP | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-01` \| Capability names and request/result shapes come from a closed, versioned registry; unknown capabilities and incompatible shapes are rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-02` \| Sealed native capabilities resolve only to their kernel-native provider and cannot be overridden through priority or profile order. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-03` \| Required cardinality is enforced: zero providers, multiple providers for a singleton or an illegal provider set fails resolution. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-04` \| Identical manifests and profile inputs always resolve the same provider set, ordering and capability-graph digest. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-05` \| Provider API version, feature, protocol and scope constraints are checked before a provider becomes callable. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-06` \| `SINGLE_PROVIDER_REQUEST` reaches one finite, replaceable, non-authoritative capability under declared deadline and cancellation bounds; it cannot target a native owner or sealed capability. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-07` \| Provider timeout, crash, cancellation, malformed result or undeclared side effect yields a typed contained failure and no authoritative mutation. \|<br>\| `CPK-CAP-08` \| Native commands, transitions, approvals, publication, grants and effects bypass capability dispatch and succeed only through their generated typed native-owner APIs. \| |
| CPK-EFF | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-EFF | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | P | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-01` \| Effects use a closed typed vocabulary; an unknown effect class or target is denied. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-02` \| Each plugin declares finite maximum effects and resources; missing, implicit, wildcard or unbounded ceilings fail validation. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-03` \| The effective grant is the intersection of manifest ceiling, resolved profile, native policy, caller scope and any exact runtime grant; no layer can widen another. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-04` \| Scope inheritance can only narrow; sibling or upward acquisition is rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-05` \| Shadow enforcement records the same would-allow/would-deny decision as the legacy path without executing, suppressing or duplicating the effect. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-06` \| Every mutating plugin effect traverses the generated typed broker; direct filesystem, process, network, Git or WorkService bypass attempts are denied. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-07` \| Consequential effects require an owner/policy-issued grant bound to caller, operation, target, request digest, expiry and single-use identity. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-08` \| Audit facts, verification results, recommendations and observations cannot be interpreted or replayed as effect or delivery grants. \|<br>\| `CPK-EFF-09` \| Execution revalidates the bound decision immediately before commit; retry/crash tests prove revocation is honored and one idempotency identity cannot produce duplicate effects. \| |
| CPK-EVT | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-EVT | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-01` \| The event registry admits exactly `COMMITTED_FACT`, `PROPOSAL`, `RUNTIME_SIGNAL` and `DIAGNOSTIC`; unknown or legacy authority-request classes are rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-02` \| Every event has a schema-valid immutable envelope containing stable identity, class, source, scope, correlation, causation, version, ordering data and payload digest. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-03` \| Only the native owner can emit a `COMMITTED_FACT`, and every such fact is traceable to its native transaction/outbox or canonical authority record. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-04` \| A proposal, runtime signal or diagnostic cannot satisfy approval, policy, transition, completion, publication, delivery or closeout requirements. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-05` \| `SINGLE_PROVIDER_REQUEST` is treated as finite non-authoritative capability RPC, not an event class or command bus, and cannot cause durable/native mutation. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-06` \| `OBSERVE` is asynchronous to the producer and serial within the deterministic subscriber partition, using native sequence for committed facts and stable ingress ordinal otherwise. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-07` \| `PARALLEL_OBSERVE` sends one immutable envelope to order-independent handlers under finite parallelism and records one all-settled result in which completion order has no semantic meaning. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-08` \| Handler/group deadlines, queues, parallelism and redelivery are positive and finite; failures are contained; committed-fact saturation marks the consumer stale for replay, proposals/diagnostics return queue-full, and only runtime signals may use declared coalescing/drop with a gap diagnostic. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-09` \| An event carrying or targeting an authoritative command, transition, approval, publication, grant or effect is rejected; the same operation succeeds only through the generated native-owner API with all current checks. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-10` \| Injection at native-commit/before-publish and publish/before-ack crash windows resumes from the native outbox/cursor, delivers the same fact identity at least once, detects gaps and proves no plugin bypass or mediator-owned authority ledger. \|<br>\| `CPK-EVT-11` \| Fault and saturation tests prove `OBSERVE` is asynchronous and serial in deterministic subscription-partition order; `PARALLEL_OBSERVE` receives one immutable envelope and produces a bounded all-settled result; handler/group deadlines, queue/parallelism/redelivery limits and backpressure are finite; handler failure is contained; committed facts replay without drop; proposals/diagnostics fail queue-full explicitly; and only runtime signals coalesce/drop with an exact gap diagnostic. \| |
| CPK-SCOPE | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-SCOPE | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-01` \| Install, project, work, session and agent identities are normalized, immutable and included in every resolved handle and decision. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-02` \| Resolution precedence is deterministic and scope inheritance can only narrow capability, effect, event and resource access. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-03` \| A child cannot acquire ancestor-only privileges or access a sibling project, work item, session or agent. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-04` \| Project/worktree path resolution rejects traversal, symlink escape, case aliasing and paths outside the canonical repository/worktree root. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-05` \| Concurrent sessions using the same provider remain isolated in state, events, caches, credentials, grants and model-visible contributions. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-06` \| Agent/session teardown revokes their handles, subscriptions, leases and pending effects; late callbacks fail with a typed stale-scope outcome. \|<br>\| `CPK-SCOPE-07` \| Rebinding, ID reuse or replay with a stale scope epoch cannot grant access to a new work, session, agent, project or worktree. \| |
| CPK-PROF | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-PROF | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E8; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-01` \| Profile schema/version validation rejects unsupported versions, malformed limits and unknown authority-sensitive fields. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-02` \| Explicit precedence and merge rules yield the same resolved profile and explanation under every input permutation. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-03` \| Resolution produces a complete immutable boot-frozen profile with finite limits and a canonical digest. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-04` \| Unknown plugins, capabilities, effects, scopes, dependencies or unsatisfied references fail closed without permissive fallback. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-05` \| Production cannot inherit development/test permissions, and ambient environment values cannot silently select or widen a profile. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROF-06` \| Explain output identifies the provenance and reason for every inclusion, exclusion, provider, limit and denial while redacting secrets and matching the resolved digest. \| |
| CPK-BOOT | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-BOOT | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E8; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-01` \| Manifest, graph, profile, policy and artifact digests are frozen before activation; authority-affecting hot reload is rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-02` \| Activation follows deterministic topological order and produces the same ordered receipt for identical inputs. \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-03` \| Any preflight validation failure occurs before the first plugin activation or effect and leaves no partially active graph. \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-04` \| Shadow, canary and enforced modes are explicit boot-frozen choices; a default-off component cannot activate through discovery alone. \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-05` \| Activation publishes atomically only after all required nodes become ready, recording digests, order, timings and status in the boot receipt. \|<br>\| `CPK-BOOT-06` \| Mid-activation failure drains/stops started nodes in reverse dependency order, and restart with the same inputs yields the same decision and explanation. \| |
| CPK-GUARD | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-GUARD | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-01` \| The generated API canonicalizes caller, native owner, scope, target, operation and request digest before policy evaluation. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-02` \| Authentication, authorization, scope, policy, approval and effect checks execute in the contractually fixed order. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-03` \| Every stage can preserve or narrow an earlier decision but cannot widen it; a denial is terminal. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-04` \| All refusals and failures return closed typed outcomes and emit a complete decision receipt rather than ambiguous exceptions or silent fallthrough. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-05` \| CLI, WebUI, model, plugin, automation and internal callers converge on the same guarded native API; direct-handler or client-library bypass fails. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-06` \| Decisions bind manifest, profile, policy, capability, effect, owner, scope and artifact versions; precommit mismatch blocks TOCTOU use. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-07` \| Retry preserves idempotency while expired, revoked, consumed, wrong-target, wrong-caller or wrong-digest grants are rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-GUARD-08` \| The native owner rechecks its invariant at commit and records every guard stage; plugins cannot intercept, suppress, reorder or forge that record. \| |
| CPK-SESS | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-SESS | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E8; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-01` \| Canonical OMP JSONL is the sole durable authority for interactive Chat/session history. \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-02` \| Every model-visible plugin contribution is admitted as a canonical JSONL record with provenance before model invocation; hidden direct provider injection is rejected. \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-03` \| Restart/replay from the same JSONL and content-addressed artifacts reconstructs the exact ordered model-visible contributions and digests. \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-04` \| Compaction preserves provenance, active constraints, unresolved decisions, authority references and event identities without inventing facts or silently dropping obligations. \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-05` \| Providers and agents cannot read, append, compact or inject into another session without an explicitly valid native scope. \|<br>\| `CPK-SESS-06` \| Plugin disable/upgrade leaves historical entries interpretable through versioned codecs and never rewrites authoritative JSONL history in place. \| |
| CPK-PROJ | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-PROJ | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E8; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-01` \| A projection consumes only canonical native facts/records and has no command, approval, effect, transition or authority-write interface. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-02` \| The sequential projection driver applies records in native order and produces identical state and digest on repeated clean replay. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-03` \| Duplicate delivery is idempotent, and projection update plus checkpoint advancement is atomic. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-04` \| A sequence gap or corrupt record marks the projection stale, prevents it being represented as current and resumes from the native cursor/outbox without cache substitution. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-05` \| Clean rebuild and migrated replay equal the live projection for the same authority history, including tombstones and withdrawals. \|<br>\| `CPK-PROJ-06` \| WebUI, Agent Hub and Fleet Manager expose freshness and query projections but route mutations only to guarded native APIs; projection-write attempts fail. \| |
| CPK-LIFE | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-LIFE | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-LIFE-01` \| Lifecycle follows the closed local runtime state machine; every illegal transition is rejected and no state is interpreted as work lifecycle/completion. \|<br>\| `CPK-LIFE-02` \| Startup is dependency-ordered, and failed startup unwinds already-started nodes in reverse dependency order. \|<br>\| `CPK-LIFE-03` \| Drain rejects new calls, bounds outstanding work and reaches stopped or quarantined state without indefinite waiting. \|<br>\| `CPK-LIFE-04` \| Authority-affecting code/config requires a new boot epoch; only explicitly declared non-authoritative development-shadow changes may hot-reload under bounded rollback. \| |
| CPK-SUP | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-SUP | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E9; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-SUP-01` \| Plugin/handler crashes are isolated from the kernel, native owners, sibling handlers and committed authority state. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUP-02` \| Restart count, retry count, backoff, jitter source and time window are finite and deterministic from the resolved profile. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUP-03` \| Budget exhaustion quarantines the component, withdraws its capabilities/subscriptions, marks dependents degraded and requires an explicit valid recovery action. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUP-04` \| The Fleet controller remains sole scheduler/process/worktree owner; Advisor and Observer run as bounded supervised consumers, remain enabled initially and are narrowed only class-by-class after shadow/canary/rollback proof. \| |
| CPK-SUPPLY | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-SUPPLY | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | P | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E3; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-SUPPLY-01` \| Source revision, package version, artifact digest and signer/provenance requirements are pinned and verified before graph admission. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUPPLY-02` \| Locked dependencies, SBOM, licenses and build provenance are generated and verified; active plugins perform no undeclared runtime dependency fetch. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUPPLY-03` \| Install/unpack rejects traversal, symlink escape, archive bombs, unsafe modes, unexpected native code and files outside the staging root. \|<br>\| `CPK-SUPPLY-04` \| Isolated repeat builds are byte-identical or carry an explained accepted variance; the manifest digest binds the exact scanned and attested artifact. \| |
| CPK-ROLLBACK | v9.1/03E qualification family CPK-ROLLBACK | OMP-204–208 / CPK-0–6; Required conjunction with mapped H gate | N | Existing native primitives do not establish complete CPK family qualification. | All cases in this family remain; preserve supply/privacy/performance/negative-authority detail. | E9; exact source prerequisites and §E authority apply | \| ID \| Required assertion \|<br>\| --- \| --- \|<br>\| `CPK-ROLLBACK-01` \| Failed profile resolution or boot activation atomically restores the last-known-good boot set and emits an exact failure/rollback receipt. \|<br>\| `CPK-ROLLBACK-02` \| Projection/profile/manifest downgrade fixtures preserve native authority and rebuild derived state; rollback never rolls back authoritative WorkService, JSONL or Git facts. \|<br>\| `CPK-ROLLBACK-03` \| Canary or legacy-retirement rollback restores routing without dual authority, duplicate effects or abandoned processes, and the emergency-disable path works without the failed plugin. \| |

## C. Before, during and after the completed goal

| Period | Demonstrated change | What it does not prove |
|---|---|---|
| Before September18 first-delivery goal | Work Ledger, native isolation/recovery, model/tool/session/TUI infrastructure, WebUI sessions/streaming/files/terminal/approval UX; protected CI/branch work; knowledge/capture/compiler/Enola/inference candidates; native budget/preflight/account/card/quote work; R02 contracts through `d49004617e` at00:56/`57ab92fbd4`00:57 | These did not spring into existence during one evening. Neither broad source volume nor old test counts establish whole integrated programme. |
| Already dirty at goal entry | R04-S1's23 files/frozen tree; existing Web progress/UI dirt | Later preservation/packaging is not authorship of all preceding work. R04 final review remained unfinished. |

| New controls/integration | OMP `96f511b4`03:43:46 adds typed control boundary/installer/tests. Web `0a2e0cd` same time combines pre-existing progress candidate with control changes | Commit's entire5,459-line Web delta is not all new marginal work for Plex fix. Components were packaged together, not installed/tested as one full system. |
| Queue/correction/completion records | Actual busy-turn event, consumed/deduplicated; source package; explicit owner removal of invented handoff/native slash-command blockers; goal completed13:34 then re-closed after continuation13:45 | Does not qualify disconnected/idle restart, parent programme or native backlog closure. |
